<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-003336 -->
# Arithmetic-rank projection envelopes: exact all-word law and a growing paired-face obstruction

Project: gd-exact-profile-20260926. Worker: max. Allocated round: 63.
Assignment: TASK.md; originating guidance: 5c4d3ccc1ebe4c55.
Plan finding: 0ddf7a58a2fd110e.

**Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED mathematical draft. No fact_id.**
No new lemma in this document has passed fact_submit. The assignment closes
the formal gate, so the sections below form one joint proof attempt, not a
chain of newly accepted facts. The original exact minimax Goal remains OPEN.

The frozen submodular profile is

    F_d(A) = |A|(d-|A|)/d,       A subset of {1,...,d}, d>=2.

There is no change of rank profile and no repair to another submodular body.
The deliverable has two parts. First it eliminates the projection oracle in
favor of an explicit interval-average, minimum/maximum and sorting recurrence
for EVERY physical word and every initial point. Second it calibrates a
growing family with d/2 genuine initial projection blocks, follows its actual
first face transition, and proves a scoped obstruction on every remaining
physical step. That family cannot provide the sought nonlinear adversarial
gain: its first update enters the quadratic region and never leaves it.

## 1. Scope, accepted inputs, and primary-source audit

We retain the definitions of W_n and of signed interpolation residuals from
accepted fact **1252b8823bb5a290**. In particular, W_n(h) is the supremum of

    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2),

over finite-dimensional Euclidean convex differentiable L-smooth functions
with an attained minimizer, with x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L) grad f(x_t).
All physical steps and all ordered interpolation rows are retained.

Accepted fact **04219554e41d9750** supplies the unchanged ALL-split benchmark:

    Omega(a,b) = (a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2,
    G(a,b) = 2a+b/2+sqrt(b^2+8ab)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

The maxima include every integer split. With lambda=1+sqrt(2), that fact
gives a finite positive Z_1 and the finite inequality

    B_(2^m-1) <= Z_1 lambda^m.                         (1)

Nothing from its conditional seed theorem is assumed. The accepted tau=1
witness 5b7ff4253c38b90a is not an ingredient of this construction. The new
unverified source-family obstruction e65119d6d7a877a9 is not used as a brick.

Primary orientation: Francis Bach, *Learning with Submodular Functions:
A Convex Optimization Perspective*, arXiv:1111.6453v2 (2013),
https://arxiv.org/html/1111.6453v2 . Downloaded HTML and extracted mathematical
text are saved in this worker's downloads/bach_1111.6453v2.{html,txt}.

Source hypothesis mapping: Definitions 2.1--2.2 concern normalized
submodularity and the base polyhedron. Propositions 3.2, 3.3, 4.1 and 4.2
relate greedy ranks, support and tight level sets. Propositions 6.1--6.2
cover concave cardinality functions. Our F_d is normalized and submodular
but NOT nondecreasing; we use the base body, not a positive polymatroid.
For Proposition 8.1 the separable term is (u_i-x_i)^2/2, strictly convex,
continuously differentiable, with derivative onto R. Section 8.3 and
Appendix A.3 provide quadratic projection and ordered-block orientation;
their proofs and Section 9.1's decomposition proof were read. A fixed
ordering gives only an approximation for a general submodular body. Here
permutation symmetry and the boundary inequalities proved below establish
exactness. Agglomeration along a proximal regularization parameter is not
an invariant for these GD steps. All needed mathematical statements are
rederived below, so no external theorem is a black box.

Matlas additionally returned 2106.11943 Lemma 9, 1909.00171 Theorem 4,
and 1411.0589 Proposition A.12. None is imported. In particular the first
hit's nondecreasing-cardinality hypothesis does not directly hold for F_d.

## 2. The actual body and its support

Write [d]={1,...,d}, let e be the all-ones vector, and put

    E_d={v in R^d: <e,v>=0},
    a_i=(d+1-2i)/d,       1<=i<=d,
    K_d={g in E_d: sum_(i in A) g_i <= F_d(A) for every A subset [d]}.

The vectors a, K_d, and all norms in this document use the ordinary
coordinate Euclidean metric. No graph metric or rank-weighted metric is
substituted. If A and B have disjoint differences of sizes p and q, expanding
the quadratic cardinality formula gives

    F_d(A)+F_d(B)-F_d(A union B)-F_d(A intersection B)=2pq/d>=0.

Thus F_d is submodular. It is zero at the empty and full sets, and strictly
positive at every proper nonempty set. Its successive cardinality increments
are exactly a_i, with sum_i a_i=0 and

    sum_(i=1)^k a_i = k(d-k)/d.                       (2)

Every permutation of a is in K_d: its largest k-coordinate sum is (2).
The singleton and complementary-singleton inequalities give
|g_i|<=(d-1)/d for g in K_d. Thus K_d is nonempty, closed and compact.

For any w, take a permutation pi with
w_(pi(1))>=...>=w_(pi(d)). Summation by parts gives, for g in K_d,

    <w,g>
      = sum_(k<d) (w_(pi(k))-w_(pi(k+1))) sum_(i<=k)g_(pi(i))
      <= sum_(k<d) (w_(pi(k))-w_(pi(k+1))) k(d-k)/d
      = sum_i a_i w_(pi(i)).                         (3)

The permutation g_(pi(i))=a_i attains equality. Consequently

    K_d = conv{all permutations of a},
    sigma_d(w):=max_(g in K_d)<w,g>
              =sum_i a_i w_(pi(i))
              =(1/d) sum_(i<j)|w_i-w_j|.             (4)

For completeness, equality of the two bodies follows without assuming a
majorization theorem. If a point g in K_d were outside the convex hull of
the permutations, let v be its closest point in that compact convex hull.
The vector w=g-v satisfies <w,t-v><=0 for every point t of the hull, by
one-sided differentiation of squared distance. Hence <w,g> exceeds the
hull's support value, contradicting (3) and attainment by a permutation.
The last formula in (4) follows by counting that a sorted coordinate at
rank i has coefficient (d-i)-(i-1)=d+1-2i in the pairwise differences.

Because -a is a permutation of a, K_d=-K_d. It has nonempty relative
interior in E_d. Indeed, for g in E_d and |A|=k,

    |sum_(i in A)g_i|
        <= ||g|| sqrt(k(d-k)/d).

Since k(d-k)>=d-1 for 1<=k<d, every centered g with
||g||<sqrt((d-1)/d) satisfies every proper constraint strictly.

## 3. One static function, paid smoothness, and the closest radius

Freeze the single function

    Phi_d(x)=max_(g in K_d){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.          (5)

This is the joint quadratic smoothing of (4). There is one common dual
vector g constrained to K_d; no separate smoothing of its individual edges
is substituted. Let P_d(x) denote the unique closest point of x in K_d.
Completing the square proves

    grad Phi_d(x)=P_d(x),
    Phi_d(x)=(||x||^2-||x-P_d(x)||^2)/2.              (6)

Here is a direct justification including differentiability. Compactness
and strict convexity of squared distance give a unique P_d(x). Its
optimality is equivalent to

    <x-P_d(x),g-P_d(x)><=0    for all g in K_d.       (7)

Sufficiency follows by expanding ||x-g||^2; necessity follows by taking
a small segment toward g. Applying (7) at x and x' and adding proves

    <x-x',P_d(x)-P_d(x')> >= ||P_d(x)-P_d(x')||^2.

Thus P_d is 1-Lipschitz. Evaluating the maximum (5) at each other's
maximizers shows that

    0 <= Phi_d(x')-Phi_d(x)-<P_d(x),x'-x>
       <= <P_d(x')-P_d(x),x'-x> <= ||x'-x||^2.

This proves differentiability, (6), and 1-smoothness. Convexity follows
from (5). Smoothness has no missing dimension factor: near zero on E_d,
P_d(x)=x, so the optimal global Lipschitz constant is exactly 1 for d>=2.
For prescribed L>0 use f=L Phi_d. Original GD with h_t/L then has exactly
the updates x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t P_d(x_t).

The complete minimizer set and its distance are

    argmin Phi_d = R e,
    min Phi_d=0,
    dist(x,argmin Phi_d)^2=||x-bar(x)e||^2,
    bar(x)=d^(-1)sum_i x_i.                          (8)

To prove the first equality, K_d subset E_d and 0 in K_d imply
Phi_d(te)=0 and Phi_d>=0. If z=x-bar(x)e is nonzero, choose small
epsilon in (0,2) such that epsilon z lies in K_d, using the relative
interior just proved. Its value in (5) is
(epsilon-epsilon^2/2)||z||^2>0. This excludes every other minimizer.
The closest point is the orthogonal projection bar(x)e, proving (8).
The mean of every GD trajectory is constant because P_d(x) is centered.

## 4. Exact projection from interval averages, with no projection oracle

Let y_1>=...>=y_d be the sorted coordinates of x and set z_i=y_i-a_i.
For 1<=p<=q<=d write

    av_(p,q)(z)=(sum_(j=p)^q z_j)/(q-p+1).

Define an explicit scalar array

    u_i = min_(1<=p<=i) max_(i<=q<=d) av_(p,q)(z).    (9)

Then the exact projection and residual, in the same sorted coordinates,
are

    g_i=y_i-u_i,      y-g=u.                         (10)

All minima and maxima in (9) are over the displayed finite integer sets.
There is no convex program, numerical projection, or unreported root solve
in (9). We prove the formula, including why this ordered calculation
satisfies ALL subset inequalities of the original body.

First consider minimizing ||u-z||^2/2 over the closed cone
u_1>=...>=u_d. Existence follows from coercivity and uniqueness from strict
convexity. For this paragraph let u denote that minimizer. Put

    R_k=sum_(i<=k)(u_i-z_i), R_0=0.

Perturbing all coordinates equally gives R_d=0. Increasing a prefix is a
feasible direction, so R_k>=0. At a strict jump u_k>u_(k+1), the same
prefix can also be decreased slightly, so R_k=0. Thus

    R_k>=0, R_d=0, R_k(u_k-u_(k+1))=0.               (11)

Conversely, a decreasing u satisfying (11) is the unique minimizer.
For any decreasing v, summation by parts gives

    <u-z,v-u>
       =sum_(k<d) R_k[(v_k-v_(k+1))-(u_k-u_(k+1))]>=0.

Adding ||v-u||^2/2 to this expression gives the squared-distance
objective difference, proving the converse.

Let [b,c] be a maximal constant block of u, with level t. At both
boundaries R_(b-1)=R_c=0, including the endpoints 0 and d. Hence

    t=av_(b,c)(z),
    sum_(j=b)^k z_j <= (k-b+1)t       (b<=k<=c),
    sum_(j=k)^c z_j >= (c-k+1)t       (b<=k<=c).      (12)

For i in this block, choose p=b in (9). Every average av_(b,q) with
q>=i is <=t: before c this follows from the prefix inequality in (12),
and after c it follows by adding full later blocks and a prefix of a
later block, all of whose levels are <=t. Equality occurs at q=c.
This gives the upper bound t in (9). Conversely, for every p<=i take
q=c. If p lies in this block, (12) gives av_(p,c)>=t. If p is earlier,
decompose [p,c] into a suffix of its first block and successive full
blocks; all their levels are >=t. This again gives av_(p,c)>=t.
Thus (9) equals t. This proves (9) for every i.

Now put g=y-u. By (2) and (11),

    sum_(i<=k)g_i=F_d({1,...,k})-R_k<=k(d-k)/d,
    sum_i g_i=0.                                    (13)

We also need g to be decreasing. Inside a constant block of u, its
differences are exactly those of y, so they are nonnegative. At the end
c of a block, (12) gives z_c>=u_c; at the first coordinate c+1 of the
next block it gives z_(c+1)<=u_(c+1). Therefore

    g_c=a_c+z_c-u_c >= a_c > a_(c+1)
          >=a_(c+1)+z_(c+1)-u_(c+1)=g_(c+1).

Consequently the prefix bounds (13) imply EVERY subset bound: the sum
over any k coordinates is no larger than the first k sorted coordinates.
So g belongs to K_d, in the real Euclidean metric.

Finally, for every v in K_d, summation by parts yields

    <u,v-g>
      =sum_(k<d)(u_k-u_(k+1)) sum_(i<=k)(v_i-g_i)<=0. (14)

At a nonzero jump in u, (11) says R_k=0, so the prefix of g in (13)
equals the body's bound, whereas that of v is at most that bound.
The total-sum term is zero. Formula (14) is exactly (7), hence g is
the true projection. Undoing the sorting permutation proves (10) for x.
Ties cause no ambiguity in the physical gradient: exchanging equal
coordinates of x preserves the problem and uniqueness fixes its projection.

An explicit form of the state on a maximal block [b,c] is

    u_i = av_(b,c)(y) - (d+1-b-c)/d,
    g_i = y_i-av_(b,c)(y)+(d+1-b-c)/d,    b<=i<=c.    (15)

Thus block coordinate differences in the projection equal the corresponding
differences of y. At a physical GD step h they change in the iterate by
the factor 1-h. In particular h>1 reverses every nonconstant old block.
Blocks may also interleave across their former boundaries. A claim that
the chronological GD blocks can only merge would therefore require an
additional argument, not a proximal-path monotonicity citation.

## 5. Explicit all-word trajectory, values, and all interpolation rows

Fix ANY finite nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), any d>=2 and any x_0
with R^2=||x_0-bar(x_0)e||^2>0. Let pi_0 list original coordinate labels
in decreasing order of x_0. At each physical t perform the following
mathematical recurrence:

    y_(t,i)=x_(t,pi_t(i)),
    z_(t,i)=y_(t,i)-a_i,
    u_(t,i)=min_(p<=i) max_(q>=i) av_(p,q)(z_t),
    v_(t,i)=(1-h_t)y_(t,i)+h_t u_(t,i),
    y_(t+1,k)=v_(t,sigma_t(k)),
    pi_(t+1)(k)=pi_t(sigma_t(k)).                     (16)

Here sigma_t sorts v_t in decreasing order; ties may be broken by the
original coordinate labels pi_t(i). For the terminal time evaluate u_n
by the same formula but apply no extra step. The gradient in physical
coordinates is

    g_(t,pi_t(i))=y_(t,i)-u_(t,i).

Equations (9)--(10) show inductively that (16) is EXACTLY
x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t grad Phi_d(x_t), with its original h_t. Sorting is an
orthogonal relabeling for that state only; pi_t lifts every coordinate
back to its physical label. No chronological step or enclosing parameter
is reordered. Means are preserved since sum u_t=sum y_t.

The true-distance normalized payoff of this single actual function is

    V_d(h,x_0)
       = [sum_i y_(n,i)^2-sum_i u_(n,i)^2]/R^2.       (17)

This is an all-word recurrence and payoff law with no remaining continuous
optimization. It is valid even if the number of blocks grows, shrinks or
changes nonmonotonically. It is not asserted to optimize over x_0 or d.
As a legal instance it gives V_d(h,x_0)<=W_n(h).

All ordered interpolation inequalities follow from the actual function,
not merely from adjacent consistency. For physical samples let

    G_i=P_d(x_i), Y_i=x_i-G_i, F_i=Phi_d(x_i).

Add the closest minimizer sample x_star=bar(x_0)e, G_star=0, F_star=0.
For EVERY ordered pair, direct substitution of
F_i=<x_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2 gives

    Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<G_i,x_j-x_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2
        =<G_i-G_j,Y_j><=0.                          (18)

The inequality is (7) at j because G_i belongs to K_d, including G_star.
At the optimum Y_star=x_star is orthogonal to K_d. Thus (18) includes
all remote, incoming-terminal, outgoing-terminal and both optimum rows.

## 6. One frozen growing calibration: every pair reverses, then enters the core

Let d=2r>=4. Define centered orthogonal vectors A and b by

    A_(2j-1)=A_(2j)=Abar_j=(d+2-4j)/d,
    b_(2j-1)=1/(2d), b_(2j)=-1/(2d),   1<=j<=r.

Freeze the primary initial point

    x_0=2A+b.                                       (19)

The body is still precisely K_d. For a useful scope extension of this
same body and pair pattern, we also prove the formulas for

    x_0=gamma A+b,             1<=gamma<=2.           (20)

This is a symbolic amplitude interval, not an executed parameter search
or a change of submodular structure. Formula (19) remains the selected
nonlinear calibration. Centering is exact. Therefore the closest initial
minimizer is zero, despite the full line of minimizers in (8).

The rank vector has a_(2j-1)=Abar_j+1/d and a_(2j)=Abar_j-1/d.
The initial point is decreasing: inside a pair its gap is 1/d, and
between pairs the gap is (4gamma-1)/d>0. Its z=x_0-a has entries

    z_(2j-1)=(gamma-1)Abar_j-1/(2d),
    z_(2j)=(gamma-1)Abar_j+1/(2d).

For gamma>1 the exact decreasing isotonic residual has r distinct blocks,
each equal to one adjacent pair, with levels (gamma-1)Abar_j. Indeed its
R_(2j-1)=1/(2d)>0, R_(2j)=0, and its block levels strictly decrease.
These are the full conditions (11). For gamma=1 all levels merge to zero.
It follows that

    G_0=A+b, Y_0=(gamma-1)A,
    F_0=(gamma-1/2)||A||^2+||b||^2/2.                (21)

For gamma>1 this is an actual nonquadratic query. Its residual Y_0 exposes
a face with r independent pair directions: the convex hull of the 2^r
vertices obtained by swapping the two ranks independently in every pair.
Each projected pair Abar_j plus/minus 1/(2d) is strictly between that
pair's endpoint ranks Abar_j plus/minus 1/d. The block state is therefore
not a formal or degenerate active-set prescription.

Let the first step be 1+alpha, with 0<alpha<1/2. The actual first iterate is

    X_1=(gamma-1-alpha)A-alpha b.                    (22)

For the frozen gamma=2, each pair has reversed. Sorting only exchanges
the two original labels in each pair, because the gap between successive
sorted pairs is (4-5alpha)/d>0. The sorted first point has entries

    y_(1,2j-1)=(1-alpha)Abar_j+alpha/(2d),
    y_(1,2j)=(1-alpha)Abar_j-alpha/(2d).

For every proper even k and odd k respectively, its exact prefix sums are

    sum_(i<=k)y_(1,i)=(1-alpha) k(d-k)/d,                     k even,
    sum_(i<=k)y_(1,i)=(1-alpha) k(d-k)/d+(-1+3alpha/2)/d,      k odd.
                                                               (23)

The identities follow by summing Abar_1,...,Abar_j, whose sum is
j(d-2j)/d, and then the possible first member of the next pair.
Since -1+3alpha/2<0, (23) puts X_1 in (1-alpha)K_d and strictly in the
relative interior of K_d. Thus G_1=X_1 and Y_1=0: all residual blocks
have merged into a single block. This is the exact nonlinear face-to-core
transition in dimension d, with a real rank reversal in every old pair.

The entire interval (20) also enters the core. The vector A is the
average of a and the permutation swapping each adjacent pair, hence A in
K_d. Also b in K_d: its total is zero and every k-coordinate sum is at
most k/(2d)<=k(d-k)/d for 1<=k<d. With t=gamma-1-alpha we have

    |t|<=1-alpha,       |t|+alpha<=1.

Central symmetry, convexity and 0 in K_d imply
tA-alpha b in K_d, proving the claim for (22). This proof does not
assume the same sorting permutation throughout the amplitude interval.

## 7. Unchanged physical hierarchy and every remaining update

For an integer m>=2 and a real tau with 0<tau<m, set

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-tau/m,
    c=lambda(1+tau/m), alpha=beta/c,
    H_(0;m,tau)=(),
    H_(j+1;m,tau)=(H_(j;m,tau),1+alpha c^j,H_(j;m,tau)), 0<=j<m.

Every child uses the SAME enclosing (m,tau). The physical word is
H_(m;m,tau), of length n=2^m-1. Tau=1 is the original ruler family;
tau=1/4 is the preferred diagnostic. No shorter relabeled word is used.
For growing dimension take d=2^m in (19), although all conclusions below
hold independently for any even d>=4.

Let nu_2(k) be the exponent of 2 dividing a positive integer k, and let
s_2(i) be the number of ones in the binary expansion of a nonnegative
integer i. The concatenation gives the exact physical step at zero-based
index t:

    h_t=1+alpha c^(nu_2(t+1)),          0<=t<n.       (24)

To check this by induction, the bridge joining two length-(2^j-1) words
is at t=2^j-1 and has nu_2(t+1)=j. The left child keeps its indices.
An index of the right child has t+1=2^j+s for 1<=s<2^j, and
nu_2(2^j+s)=nu_2(s), since the power of 2 dividing s is smaller than 2^j.
This reproduces the same child steps with the same enclosing parameters;
the base case is the empty word followed by its first bridge.

For a prefix of i steps, count each power of 2 dividing the integers
1,...,i. This gives

    sum_(k=1)^i nu_2(k)=sum_(ell>=1)floor(i/2^ell)=i-s_2(i).

For the second equality, write i as sum_j epsilon_j 2^j; each binary
digit contributes epsilon_j(2^j-1) to the sum of floors. Therefore the
exact unit-quadratic prefix product is

    P_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t)=(-1)^i beta^i c^(-s_2(i)).       (25)

In particular P_1=-alpha. For i>=1 define

    sigma_i=P_i/P_1=(-1)^(i-1) beta^(i-1)c^(1-s_2(i)).        (26)

Every i>=1 has s_2(i)>=1. Since 0<beta<1 and c>1,

    |sigma_i|<=1                 for every 1<=i<=n.          (27)

This is a statement about EVERY physical prefix, including those that
cross a large checkpoint. It is not merely a terminal product estimate.

Section 6 proved X_1 in K_d and the true first update. Equations (26)--(27)
and the central symmetry of K_d now prove the complete remaining trajectory
by induction:

    x_0=gamma A+b,  G_0=A+b,
    x_i=sigma_i X_1, G_i=x_i, F_i=||x_i||^2/2, Y_i=0,
                                                1<=i<=n.  (28)

Indeed x_i in K_d implies P_d(x_i)=x_i; the next original update is
(1-h_i)x_i=sigma_(i+1)X_1, and (27) puts that next point in K_d too.
There is no rescaling or forgetting of old planes, and no induction on a
surrogate child parameter. For the nonlinear gamma=2 start there are
exactly r initial residual blocks, a first pairwise rank reversal, and
then a single residual block for the rest of the actual word.

One can check every original interpolation row even more explicitly.
For i>=1, or i=star, rows whose second index j is >=1 or star have
Q_ij=0, and the same holds for Q_(0,j) at those j. The remaining rows are

    Q_(i,0)=(gamma-1)[sigma_i(gamma-1-alpha)-1]||A||^2<=0,
                                                   1<=i<=n,
    Q_(star,0)=-(gamma-1)||A||^2<=0.                         (29)

Here |sigma_i|<=1 and |gamma-1-alpha|<=1-alpha<1. Formula (29), together
with (18), pays all remote and terminal interpolation conditions.

## 8. Exact radius, payoff, and same-word obstruction

Orthogonality of A and b follows separately in each pair. Summing their
squares gives

    ||A||^2=(d^2-4)/(3d),  ||b||^2=1/(4d),
    R_gamma^2=gamma^2||A||^2+||b||^2
             =[4gamma^2(d^2-4)+3]/(12d).                    (30)

For the first identity, substitute d=2r and use
sum_(j=1)^r(r+1-2j)^2=r(r^2-1)/3, obtained by expanding the square and
the finite sums of j and j^2. This is textual algebra, not an executed
arithmetic check. R_gamma in (30) is the distance to the ENTIRE minimum
line in (8), not the norm to an arbitrary designated minimizer.

Let

    J_(d,gamma)(alpha)
      = [4(gamma-1-alpha)^2(d^2-4)+3alpha^2]
          /[4gamma^2(d^2-4)+3].                            (31)

This is exactly ||X_1||^2/R_gamma^2 by (22) and (30).
Since s_2(n)=m, (26) and (28) give the actual normalized terminal payoff

    V_(m,tau,d,gamma)
       =J_(d,gamma)(alpha) beta^(2n-2)c^(2-2m).              (32)

For the frozen initial point gamma=2 the explicit expressions are

    R_2^2=(16d^2-61)/(12d),
    J_(d,2)(alpha)
       =[4(1-alpha)^2(d^2-4)+3alpha^2]/(16d^2-61).           (33)

These are exact finite formulas for unbounded d and m; in particular
they apply to d=2^m without a dimension-dependent smoothness loss.

On the EXACT SAME physical word, the scalar function q(x)=x^2/2,
started at x_0=1, is a legal normalized instance with payoff

    Qunit_(m,tau)=P_n^2=beta^(2n)c^(-2m).                   (34)

There is no appeal to an exponent proxy or a reference on a different
word. Dividing (32) by (34) gives the exact comparison

    V_(m,tau,d,gamma)/Qunit_(m,tau)
       =J_(d,gamma)(alpha)/alpha^2.                        (35)

The sign of J_(d,gamma)(alpha)-alpha^2 is the sign of

    (gamma-1-alpha)^2-alpha^2 gamma^2
       =(gamma-1)(1+alpha)[(1-alpha)gamma-(1+alpha)],        (36)

because ||A||^2>0 for d>=4 and the b terms cancel. If alpha>1/3,
the bracket is strictly negative for 1<=gamma<=2. Therefore

    V_(m,tau,d,gamma)<Qunit_(m,tau)  for 1<gamma<=2,
    V_(m,tau,d,1)=Qunit_(m,tau).                            (37)

For 0<tau<=1/4 and m>=3, tau/m<=1/12, so

    alpha >= 11/(13lambda)>1/3.

The last strict inequality is 20>13sqrt(2), whose squares are 400>338.
Thus (37) holds on the entire specified small-tau hierarchy, for EVERY
even dimension d>=4 and every nonlinear amplitude in (1,2]. On the
original tau=1 family it holds at least for every m>=10, because then
alpha>=9/(11lambda)>1/3, the latter inequality following from
16>11sqrt(2), or 256>242 after squaring.

Combining (1), (34) and (37) gives the finite same-word benchmark bound

    B_n V_(m,tau,d,gamma)
        <= Z_1 lambda^(-m) beta^(2n)(1+tau/m)^(-2m),        (38)

with strict inequality for gamma>1 under the domains just stated.
In particular, the supremum over ALL even d>=4 and 1<=gamma<=2 in this
proved family tends to zero as m tends to infinity at fixed tau=1/4,
and also at tau=1. This is an upper bound on the values of these actual
constructed instances, not an upper bound on W_n. It is compatible with
different functions giving much larger same-word lower witnesses.

## 9. A larger precisely scoped obstruction: first-update core entry

The mechanism behind (38) extends beyond the selected start. Fix this K_d,
the same hierarchy, and ANY initial x_0 with positive closest distance R.
Suppose its actual first point has centered part

    x_1-bar(x_0)e in K_d.                                  (39)

Translation along e leaves gradients and values unchanged. Applying the
argument in (26)--(28) to that centered point proves the exact formula

    V = (||x_1-bar(x_0)e||^2/R^2)
                       beta^(2n-2)c^(2-2m).               (40)

The prefactor is at most 1. To see this, write z=x_0-bar(x_0)e and
g=P_d(x_0). From (7) with the feasible point 0,
<z,g>>=||g||^2. Since h_0=1+alpha is in (0,2),

    ||z-h_0 g||^2
      <= ||z||^2-h_0(2-h_0)||g||^2 <= ||z||^2.

Hence every such start, in every dimension, obeys the uniform bound

    B_n V <= Z_1 lambda^m beta^(2n-2)c^(2-2m)
           <= Z_1 lambda^(2-m).                            (41)

The last inequality uses m>=2, c>=lambda and beta<=1.
Condition (39) is essential. Nothing here proves it for every initial
point of this body. Thus (41) does not close the entire permutahedral
envelope family; it closes its explicitly described first-update-core
subclass. A future candidate on the same rank profile must avoid that
subclass and must control its later genuine block transitions. Formula
(16) gives an exact starting law for that remaining question.

## 10. What is established at draft level, and what is not

The joint argument provides the following self-contained candidate results:

1. The frozen concave cardinality body, actual joint smoothing, exact
   Euclidean projection formula, full minimum set, and normalization.
2. An explicit all-word interval-average recurrence with the physical
   permutation lift, terminal payoff and all ordered interpolation rows.
3. A dimension-growing family with r=d/2 actual initial active blocks,
   exact first-step rank reversals, and every original update thereafter.
4. Exact closest radii and finite payoff formulas; dominance by the actual
   same-word unit quadratic on the stated small-tau and original domains.
5. A uniform obstruction for the larger, explicitly conditional class
   whose first physical update enters the quadratic core.

The optimistic candidate claim tested was that the paired-rank start
could supply nonlinear amplification over the unit quadratic on the
small-tau diagnostic. Equations (35)--(37) refute that claim for this
actual family. Having many initial active blocks alone does not prevent
their immediate collapse. No conclusion excludes a different start that
stays outside K_d, another rank profile, or another submodular structure.

After the first update the selected trajectory is scalar, and already
the explicit initial data span only A and b. This is the demonstrated
failure mechanism, not evidence of a nonlinear gain. The unrestricted
all-word recurrence in Section 5 imposes no such low-dimensional ansatz.
There is no claimed optimality over all starts or all polytopes, no
all-word minimax comparison, and no original-Goal completion.

The current round has used no repair. Its ONE selected mathematical
family has been carried through to an exact scoped dead end; the broader
body retains the exact recurrence and its expressly open non-core regime.
No new allocation is inferred from that open regime.

## Local notation index

F_d: frozen set function |A|(d-|A|)/d; Phi_d: its static smooth envelope.
K_d: its submodular base polytope; E_d: the centered Euclidean hyperplane.
a_i: decreasing arithmetic rank increment; sigma_d: body's support function.
P_d: Euclidean projection onto K_d. e: all-ones vector. bar(x): coordinate mean.
y: sorted iterate; z=y-a; u: residual given by (9); R_k: isotonic prefix
residual in (11), distinct from the positive initial distance R or R_gamma.
pi_t: current rank-to-physical-label permutation; sigma_t in (16): sorting
permutation for the next iterate, distinct from scalar sigma_i in (26).
A,b,Abar_j: frozen centered pair vectors and pair means in Section 6.
gamma: amplitude in the supplementary interval [1,2]; the primary value is 2.
lambda,beta,c,alpha,m,tau,n: hierarchy parameters fixed in Section 7.
nu_2: exponent of 2 in a positive integer; s_2: binary digit count.
P_i: unit-quadratic prefix product, distinct from projection P_d.
J_(d,gamma): exact first-point norm ratio; V: true-distance payoff of the
actual selected envelope instance; Qunit: actual scalar-quadratic same-word
payoff. C_n,B_n,Z_1,W_n retain the accepted definitions in Section 1.

# R8 tangent-disk handoff

2026-10-09. **All new mathematics UNVERIFIED. Original exact-profile target OPEN.**

Allocation `grantf27a906e67edabf8`: one invocation completed; one substantive disk-construction line used; optional second line unused and not carried forward. Mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions zero. No additional agent/model, private worker memory, worker/service/truth-store action, Goal mutation, publication, finalization, revocation, or shutdown.

Read PROOF_ATTEMPT.md in this folder for the full self-contained derivation. Its principal usable result is an actual original-radius ceiling, not a whole-disk ceiling:

For K=B(e1,1), F(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2), the complete minimum set is the negative horizontal ray. For x0=(1-u0,v0), u0>=1 and v0 nonzero, the closest original minimizer is (1-u0,0), so R0^2=v0^2. With r=sqrt(u^2+v^2), delta=r-u, q=r+u, the same global function has

    F=delta, g=(delta/r,v/r),
    u_next=u+h*delta/r, v_next=(1-h/r)*v,
    r_next^2=r^2+2*h*delta*(h/r-1), q_next>=q,
    E=2F_end/v0^2=(2/q_end)*product_t(1-h_t/r_t)^2.

All these equations, convexity, global 1-smoothness, full mincone and normal constraints are derived in the proof. The original radius debt is

    ||x_t-x_star||^2=(r_t-u0)^2+2*u0*delta_t.

For ANY complete finite nonnegative word with S=sum h>0 and b=max h, if q0>=2b then every r_t>=b and r_t<=r0. Consequently

    E <= (2/q0)*exp(-2*S/r0) <= 1/(e*S).

On the literal inherited H_m, S_m>3, hence D_m E<7/8. Using the accepted contextual comparison B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50, this gives B E<329/400 for every m>=1536 in that entire initial-point sector. The bound retains all physical steps, the true final iterate, the fixed enclosing-m parameters, and the original minimizer/start.

A concrete curved full-word orbit is u0=v0=3S_m. Its tangential curvature 1/r_t strictly increases at every physical step, and its actual payoff satisfies

    E >= 4/(27*sqrt(2)*S_m), D_m*E > 8/(27*sqrt(2)).

This is only a subtarget constant and belongs to the deficient sector above. It confirms actual changing curvature without pretending that drift supplies the target gain.

For each fixed word, the limit v0->0 at fixed u0>=1 is exactly

    E -> (1/u0)*product_t(1-h_t/u0)^2.

Thus infinitesimal transverse starts recover the scalar-quadratic pool. Conditional on the separate UNVERIFIED R6 bound 9/(16D_m), a fixed-m continuity argument excludes a small neighborhood |v0|<=eta_m uniformly over 1<=u0<=b_m, with E<=3/(4D_m). No quantitative or uniform-in-m control of eta_m was obtained.

The SAME disk already supplies the standard Huber payoff without an added vertex: on the positive axis, F(z,0)=z^2/2 for 0<=z<=2 and 2z-2 for z>=2. The original start x0=2D_m e1, closest min0, remains affine throughout and gives E=1/D_m. Since every H_m step is >1, any earlier quadratic-axis visit reaches the minimum ray at the next step. Checking the possible final quadratic visit proves the exact supremum over ALL axis starts is 1/D_m. No radius v0^2 is used for these positive-axis starts.

Unresolved sectors:

- Left starts with q0<2b_m, including finite-amplitude reflecting histories. Necessarily 1<=u0<b_m and |v0|<2 sqrt(b_m(b_m-1)); the fixed-m infinitesimal neighborhood is conditionally excluded as above. The coupled product has no target-scale lower bound and no whole-sector upper bound.
- Starts with positive horizontal coordinate and nonzero vertical coordinate, with their original radius ||x0|| and genuine interior/exterior transitions.
- Every arbitrary-word exact-profile selection requirement of the original problem.

The optional vertex-plus-disk repair was not used: a constant-gradient plateau is already present in the original disk, and no quantified mixed plateau/curved witness exceeding it was established. # R8 tangent-disk adversary: original-radius sector bounds

**UNVERIFIED PROOF ATTEMPT — 2026-10-09.** Every new mathematical statement below is a manual draft, not a verifier-accepted Danus fact. The original exact-profile problem remains OPEN. This attempt does not establish the required nonlinear witness on the entire inherited family, and does not establish an upper bound for the whole tangent-disk family or the full smooth-convex class.

Allocation: `grantf27a906e67edabf8`; one analytical invocation, one substantive construction line used, optional second line unused. Mathematical programs, numerical arrays, solvers, formal submissions, additional agents/models, services, workers, and truth-store changes: zero. The calculations below are handwritten derivations. File reads and artifact writes are administrative actions.

## 1. Target and outcome

For a prescribed nonnegative word h of length n, use the original normalization

\[
 W_n(h)=\sup_{f,x_0,x_\star}
 \frac{2(f(x_n)-f_\star)}{\|x_0-x_\star\|^2},\qquad L=1.
\]

The supremum includes all finite dimensions, globally convex differentiable functions with 1-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum, and actual selected minimizers and starts. The physical update is x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t grad f(x_t). Write B_n=2 S_bullet(n)+1, where S_bullet is the exact all-integer-split sequence in the original PROBLEM.md. The original positive target is a word-independent threshold for W_n(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n for every prescribed word, not merely a result on the family below.

For every enclosing m>=1536, keep its parameters fixed throughout

\[
 \epsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad \beta=1-\epsilon,\quad
 c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\epsilon),\quad a=\beta/c,
\]
\[
 H_0=\varnothing,\qquad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j),\quad
 S_j=\sum H_j,\quad D_m=1+2S_m,\quad n_m=2^m-1.
\]

TASK.md and the current R7 handoff quote accepted fact `931b9653ce0f7201` as B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50. That comparison is an external accepted input here; no new fact-store lookup or formal submission was made.

This attempt obtains the following original-radius results for the single fixed disk envelope:

1. For every complete nonnegative word with mass S>0 and maximum step b, every left-side start satisfying q_0=r_0+u_0>=2b has payoff at most 1/(e S). On the literal H_m this is less than 7/(8D_m), and hence less than (329/400)/B_(n_m).
2. A concrete genuinely curved trajectory exists on every complete H_m: u_0=v_0=3S_m. Its original-radius payoff is at least 4/(27 sqrt(2) S_m), but it lies in the preceding deficient sector. Its actual tangential curvature changes at every physical step.
3. The same disk already supplies exactly the standard 1/D_m Huber payoff on its positive horizontal ray. In fact, every horizontal-axis start is bounded by 1/D_m for H_m.
4. Infinitesimal transverse starts limit to the scalar-quadratic pool. Conditional on the separate unverified R6 quadratic bound, these starts are also deficient. This limit is not uniform in m without additional work.

The remaining finite-amplitude reflecting sector is not bounded or made into a target-scale witness. Starts with a positive horizontal coordinate and nonzero vertical coordinate also remain open. Consequently there is no whole-family viability result, and no arbitrary-word closure.

## 2. One global function and its full minimum set

Let e=(1,0), K={g: ||g-e||<=1}, and

\[
 F(x)=\max_{g\in K}\{\langle x,g\rangle-\tfrac12\|g\|^2\}.
\]

Completing the square gives

\[
 F(x)=\tfrac12\|x\|^2-\tfrac12\operatorname{dist}(x,K)^2,
 \qquad g(x)=P_Kx.
\]

The maximum is attained uniquely because K is compact and the objective is strictly concave in g. A maximum of affine functions of x is convex. To check differentiability directly, let p=P_Kx and p_z=P_K(x+z). Comparing each maximizer against the other gives

\[
 \langle p,z\rangle\le F(x+z)-F(x)
 \le\langle p_z,z\rangle.
\]

Projection optimality gives <x-p,q-p><=0 for q in K. Using this for two projections and adding yields

\[
 \|P_Kx-P_Ky\|^2\le
 \langle P_Kx-P_Ky,x-y\rangle.
\]

Thus P_K is 1-Lipschitz. The preceding difference inequalities show F is differentiable with gradient P_K and that its gradient is globally 1-Lipschitz. In particular, the piecewise formula used below defines one admissible function on all of R^2.

Since 0 belongs to K, F>=0 and F(0)=0. Moreover P_Kx=0 exactly when <x,g><=0 for every g in K. The support function of K is x_1+||x||. Therefore

\[
 \arg\min F=\{(z,0):z\le0\},\qquad F_\star=0.
\]

This is the complete minimum set. The closest minimizer to (x_1,x_2) is (x_1,0) when x_1<=0, and is 0 when x_1>=0. A different minimizer only increases the denominator for this fixed start, so it cannot improve this particular function's normalized payoff.

For r=||x-e|| the exact global formula is

\[
 \begin{array}{ll}
 r\le1:& g=x,\quad F(x)=\|x\|^2/2,\\[2mm]
 r\ge1:& g=e+(x-e)/r,\quad F(x)=r+x_1-1.
 \end{array}
\]

The formulas agree on the boundary. They follow either from the projection formula for a ball or directly from the completed square above.

## 3. Exact left-side dynamics and the original radius

Write

\[
 x=(1-u,v),\quad u\ge1,\quad
 r=\sqrt{u^2+v^2},\quad \delta=r-u,\quad q=r+u.
\]

This point is outside the open disk; it is strictly outside when v is nonzero. The preceding global formula gives

\[
 F(x)=\delta,\qquad
 g(x)=(\delta/r,v/r),\qquad v^2=\delta q.
\]

Every gradient of F has a nonnegative first component, because K is contained in {g_1>=0}. Thus every nonnegative physical step preserves u>=u_0>=1. Substituting the gradient, without changing h, gives

\[
 u'=u+h\delta/r,\qquad v'=(1-h/r)v.
 \tag{3.1}
\]

Expanding the two squares gives

\[
 r'^2-r^2
 =2h\left(\frac{u\delta-v^2}{r}\right)
   +h^2\frac{\delta^2+v^2}{r^2}
 =2h\delta(h/r-1),
 \tag{3.2}
\]

where u delta-v^2=-r delta and delta^2+v^2=2r delta.

The claimed monotonicity of q needs a separate check. Set A=r-h delta/r. Then

\[
 r'^2-A^2=\frac{h^2\delta q}{r^2}
             =\frac{h^2v^2}{r^2}\ge0.
\]

If A>=0, this implies r'>=A; if A<0, that inequality follows from r'>=0. Hence

\[
 q'-q=r'-r+h\delta/r\ge0.
 \tag{3.3}
\]

It is strict if h>0 and v is nonzero. In contrast, r need not be monotone: (3.2) gives its direction of change exactly. The varying curvature is therefore coupled to this same trajectory.

For v_0 nonzero the closest original minimizer is

\[
 x_\star=(1-u_0,0),\qquad R_0^2=\|x_0-x_\star\|^2=v_0^2.
\]

Using (3.1) through the true final time N gives the exact normalized payoff

\[
 E(h;u_0,v_0):=\frac{2F(x_N)}{v_0^2}
 =\frac{2}{q_N}\prod_{t=0}^{N-1}(1-h_t/r_t)^2.
 \tag{3.4}
\]

If a factor vanishes, the trajectory reaches the minimum ray and remains there, and (3.4) still gives zero. No post-step radius is used as the denominator.

Two further identities make the original-radius accounting explicit:

\[
 \|x_t-x_\star\|^2
 =(u_t-u_0)^2+v_t^2
 =(r_t-u_0)^2+2u_0\delta_t,
 \tag{3.5}
\]
\[
 \langle g_t,x_t-x_\star\rangle
 =\delta_t+\frac{u_0}{2}\|g_t\|^2,
 \qquad \|g_t\|^2=2\delta_t/r_t.
 \tag{3.6}
\]

For (3.6), its numerator is (u_0-u_t)delta_t+v_t^2=delta_t(u_0+r_t). Thus the horizontal drift remains a real distance cost relative to the originally selected minimizer.

For completeness, the global normal geometry also follows directly. Put s_t=(-u_t,v_t)/r_t. Then g_t=e+s_t, x_t-g_t=(r_t-1)s_t, and ||s_t||=1. For two trajectory samples,

\[
 \langle x_i-g_i,g_i-g_j\rangle
 =(r_i-1)(1-\langle s_i,s_j\rangle)
 =\tfrac12(r_i-1)\|g_i-g_j\|^2\ge0.
 \tag{3.7}
\]

This checks all disk supporting normals at once; no interpolation point is freely activated. The same formula includes the original minimizer as g_star=0, r_star=u_0, s_star=-e. On the exterior, differentiation gives the Hessian (I-s s^T)/r, whose nonzero tangential eigenvalue is 1/r.

## 4. A complete-word ceiling when q_0>=2 max h

**Draft sector theorem.** Let h be any finite nonnegative word with S=sum h>0 and b=max h. For the fixed disk and a left-side start with u_0>=1, v_0 nonzero, and q_0>=2b,

\[
 E(h;u_0,v_0)\le\frac1{eS}.
 \tag{4.1}
\]

Indeed r_t=(q_t+delta_t)/2>=q_t/2>=q_0/2>=b. Therefore every multiplier 1-h_t/r_t lies in [0,1]. Equation (3.2) also yields r_(t+1)<=r_t, so r_t<=r_0 throughout the complete word. By 1-z<=exp(-z) for z>=0,

\[
 \prod_t(1-h_t/r_t)^2
 \le\exp(-2\sum_t h_t/r_t)
 \le\exp(-2S/r_0).
\]

Equations (3.3)--(3.4) and q_0>=r_0 now give

\[
 E\le\frac2{q_0}e^{-2S/r_0}
 \le\frac2{r_0}e^{-2S/r_0}
 =\frac1S z e^{-z}\le\frac1{eS},
 \qquad z=2S/r_0.
\]

The last scalar maximum follows by differentiating z exp(-z), whose derivative is (1-z)exp(-z). A zero multiplier is already covered. Zero entries of h cause no difficulty.

For H_m, S_1=1+a and S_2=3+2a+beta>3, so S_m>3 for m>=2. Consequently

\[
 D_m E\le\frac{2+1/S_m}{e}
 <\frac7{3e}<\frac78.
 \tag{4.2}
\]

Here e>8/3 follows from the positive exponential series, whose terms through degree three sum to 8/3. Combining (4.2) with the accepted input B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50 gives

\[
 B_{n_m}E<\frac{329}{400}<1.
 \tag{4.3}
\]

This is an original-scale failure on every complete inherited word, uniformly over this whole start sector. It is not an upper bound for starts outside the sector.

The sector is substantial relative to the largest physical step. The exact mass, obtained by unrolling S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+a c^j and summing the geometric progression, is

\[
 S_m=2^m-1+a\frac{c^m-2^m}{c-2},\qquad
 b_m=1+a c^{m-1}.
\]

As the enclosing m tends to infinity with its prescribed parameters, c tends to 1+sqrt(2), a tends to its reciprocal, and (2/c)^m tends to zero. Thus

\[
 b_m/S_m\longrightarrow(c_0-2)/c_0=3-2\sqrt2.
\]

This asymptotic observation is not used in (4.2)--(4.3); those bounds hold for the literal finite words and parameters.

## 5. An explicit full-word curved trajectory, with its deficient payoff

The ceiling is not based on forcing a constant curvature. For every H_m with S=S_m, choose the explicit original start and minimizer

\[
 u_0=v_0=3S,\qquad
 x_0=(1-3S,3S),\qquad x_\star=(1-3S,0).
\]

The original radius is R_0=3S. Every physical h_t is at most S, while r_t>=u_t>=u_0=3S. Hence every multiplier lies in (0,1), v_t stays nonzero, and, since each H_m entry is positive, (3.2) gives r_(t+1)<r_t at every step. The tangential curvature 1/r_t strictly increases at each of the n_m physical steps. Equation (3.3) also gives a strictly increasing q_t.

At the same time,

\[
 \frac{v_N}{v_0}
 =\prod_t(1-h_t/r_t)
 \ge\prod_t(1-h_t/(3S))\ge1-\sum_t h_t/(3S)=2/3.
\]

The product inequality follows by induction from (1-a)(1-b)>=1-a-b for nonnegative a,b with sum at most one. Also q_N<=2r_N<=2r_0=6sqrt(2)S. Substitution in (3.4) yields the actual terminal bound

\[
 E(H_m;3S_m,3S_m)\ge\frac4{27\sqrt2\,S_m},
 \qquad
 D_m E>\frac8{27\sqrt2}.
 \tag{5.1}
\]

This construction is finite-dimensional, uses one global function and the full word, and has genuine curvature drift. Its payoff is only a fixed subtarget constant. The sector ceiling (4.3) excludes this entire choice of large initial scales from reaching 1/B_(n_m); (5.1) is not claimed as the requested target-scale closure.

## 6. The small transverse limit and its precise scope

For any fixed complete word and fixed u_0>=1, the exterior formulas are smooth in (u,v) near the invariant trajectory (u_0,0). Induction in the finite number of physical steps gives, as v_0 tends to zero,

\[
 u_t=u_0+O(v_0^2),\quad r_t=u_0+O(v_0^2),\quad
 v_t=v_0\prod_{s<t}(1-h_s/u_0)+O(v_0^3).
\]

The constants can depend on the entire word. Using (3.4),

\[
 \lim_{v_0\to0} E(h;u_0,v_0)
 =\frac1{u_0}\prod_t(1-h_t/u_0)^2.
 \tag{6.1}
\]

For u_0>=1, lambda=1/u_0 lies in (0,1]. Thus this limit is exactly the admissible scalar-quadratic payoff, with no rescaled physical steps. Taking u_0 large covers the endpoint limit lambda=0 as well.

The separate file R6_QUADRATIC_BASELINE_BOUND_20261009.md is an **unverified** manual proof that the entire scalar-quadratic pool on this same inherited H_m is at most 9/(16D_m). Conditional on that draft, (6.1) is at most this value for every u_0.

There is a limited uniform statement at each fixed m. On the compact interval 1<=u_0<=b_m, the finite compositions in (3.1) and the expression (3.4) have a continuous extension to v_0=0. Their convergence in (6.1) is therefore uniform on this compact interval. Conditional on the R6 draft, for each fixed m there exists eta_m>0 such that

\[
 1\le u_0\le b_m,\quad 0<|v_0|\le\eta_m
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad E(H_m;u_0,v_0)\le\frac3{4D_m}.
 \tag{6.2}
\]

For u_0>=b_m, the sector theorem already applies since q_0>=2u_0. No estimate on eta_m as m grows has been obtained. In particular, the fixed-m continuity argument cannot exclude m-dependent transverse amplitudes outside this neighborhood or be treated as a uniform asymptotic theorem.

## 7. The same disk's exact horizontal-axis baseline

Other starts require their actual minimum distance. On the horizontal axis the global function in section 2 reduces to

\[
 F(z,0)=
 \begin{cases}
 0,&z\le0,\\
 z^2/2,&0\le z\le2,\\
 2z-2,&z\ge2.
 \end{cases}
\]

For any nonnegative word of mass S, put D=1+2S and x_0=2D e. The closest minimizer is 0, so R_0^2=4D^2. Every physical iterate is x_t=2(D-prefix_mass_t)e and lies in the affine region. The true terminal point is 2(S+1)e and F(x_N)=2D. Hence

\[
 \frac{2F(x_N)}{R_0^2}=\frac1D.
 \tag{7.1}
\]

For H_m, every physical step is greater than one. This permits an exact upper bound on the entire horizontal-axis family, including crossings. A nonpositive iterate is already a minimum and remains there. An iterate z in (0,2] is mapped by the next step h>1 to (1-h)z<0. Thus a nonzero terminal objective requires all earlier iterates to remain in the affine region, except that the final point itself may be in [0,2]. In that event z_0=z_N+2S_m and the original minimizer is 0.

If z_N>=2, the payoff is

\[
 \frac{4z_N-4}{(z_N+2S_m)^2}\le\frac1{1+2S_m},
\]

as differentiation gives the maximum at z_N=2(S_m+1). If 0<=z_N<=2, it is

\[
 \frac{z_N^2}{(z_N+2S_m)^2}
 \le\frac1{(S_m+1)^2}\le\frac1{1+2S_m}.
\]

An earlier quadratic visit gives zero, as already explained. Therefore, for every complete H_m,

\[
 \sup_{\text{horizontal-axis starts, minimizers}}
 \frac{2F(x_N)}{\|x_0-x_\star\|^2}=\frac1{D_m}.
 \tag{7.2}
\]

The supremum can restrict to closest minimizers because F>=0. The accepted comparison gives B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50, so this exact baseline still misses the original target.

## 8. What remains, and why no vertex repair was claimed

For a left-side start to escape the unconditional sector ceiling, it must satisfy

\[
 u_0\ge1,\qquad 0<q_0<2b_m.
\]

Since q_0>=2u_0 and v_0^2=q_0(q_0-2u_0), this entails

\[
 1\le u_0<b_m,\qquad
 0<|v_0|<2\sqrt{b_m(b_m-1)}.
\]

Conditional on the separate R6 draft, the fixed-m infinitesimal neighborhood in (6.2) can also be removed. The residual sector consists of finite transverse amplitudes at smaller initial q_0, where long physical steps may reflect v and change r. No bound on the full product in (3.4), relative to the original v_0, was obtained there. It must not be replaced by an optimized free sequence of r_t.

The theorem in section 4 does not cover every left-side trajectory that happens to avoid a reflection, nor does failure of its initial condition prove that a reflection occurs. It is a sufficient condition with a precise quantitative conclusion. No inference about the complement is made.

Starts with x_(0,1)>0 and x_(0,2) nonzero are a further unexcluded sector. Their closest minimizer is 0 and their original radius is ||x_0||. They may have disk-interior transitions before reaching the left region. A left-region formula cannot be attached to such a trajectory by resetting its radius.

The optional exposed-vertex repair was not used. The disk itself already has an exact constant-gradient Huber plateau, by section 7. Adding a vertex merely to supply such a plateau would not address a missing mechanism. A useful second construction would have to supply a quantified mixed plateau/curved trajectory exceeding the baseline on complete H_m words, while proving its connecting projection geometry and paying the prefix radius. No such quantitative candidate was established in this invocation. A geometric parametrization alone would not meet the task's decisive test.

Even a future target-scale witness for all H_m would only pass the specified family test. The original problem would still require an all-word selection theorem with the exact B_n and a schedule-independent asymptotic threshold. Conversely, the restricted upper bounds here say nothing about a full-class asymptotic separation.

## 9. Evidence and closeout

Read completely: current OPERATOR.md, original PROBLEM.md (including the tail after the first output was truncated), this allocation's TASK.md, R7_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261009.md, and R6_QUADRATIC_BASELINE_BOUND_20261009.md. The supplied memory registry was used only to locate the project's scope; the current operator file and live task govern the resumed authorization. The mathematical-academic-writing integrity instructions were used to check quantifiers, normalization, and evidence levels.

All parent disk formulas actually used were derived in sections 2--3. The extra inverse-map/area-preservation lead was not used as a target argument. No numerical or symbolic mathematical execution occurred. No private worker memory was read, no other agent/model was called, and no project services, workers, facts, Goal, or publication state were changed.

Final status: **original target OPEN; entire-disk H_m viability OPEN; complete quantitative exclusion of the sector q_0>=2 max h; exact horizontal baseline; no complete target-relevant candidate for formal submission.** The invocation stops here without automatic renewal.

# R8: a curved projection body with actual changing tangential curvature

Fresh ONE gpt-6-astra/ultra analytical invocation, <=TWO substantive proof lines, mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions ZERO. Consult OFF. Parent owns budget. Read OPERATOR.md, full original PROBLEM.md and research/R7_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261009.md. All old grants closed. Human prioritizes direct original-target closure inspired by Zhang--Jiang; equivalent formulations and fixed-step toys are not substitutes.

The original goal remains lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ=1, W=2R, exact B_n all-integer-split profile; or a full-class asymptotic separation. For a positive answer the threshold must be uniform over EVERY nonnegative prescribed word. Preserve all finite dimensions, attained minima, original selected radius and true final iterate. This task attempts an actual nonlinear lower witness, not another representation theorem.

## Proposed one fixed geometric function

Let K be the Euclidean disk B(e1,1) in R2, tangent to0, and use

    F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2), grad F_K=P_K.

Derive its global convexity,1-smoothness and full minimum cone; do not treat the following parent manual formulas as accepted. The proposed min set is the negative horizontal ray{(-s,0):s>=0}. Its closest initial point must be used honestly if exploiting that radius.

For x=(1-u,v) with u>=1, put r=sqrt(u^2+v^2), delta=r-u. For v!=0 this stays outside the disk. Because every gradient has nonnegative first component, all nonnegative physical steps preserve u>=1. The exact proposed equations are

    F_K(x)=r-u,
    grad F_K(x)=(1-u/r,v/r),
    u_next=u+h*(1-u/r), v_next=(1-h/r)*v,
    r_next^2=r^2+2*h*delta*(h/r-1).

The ORIGINAL closest minimum at initialization is (1-u0,0), so the initial squared radius is v0^2; this is not a reset at later times. Algebra suggests r+u is nondecreasing and the exact terminal payoff is

    2F_end/v0^2 = 2/(r_end+u_end)*product_t(1-h_t/r_t)^2.

The r_t are ACTUAL coupled radii of the same trajectory, not freely selectable spectral parameters. The novelty over the rejected common-normal and piecewise-quadratic returns is this coupled drift of the tangential curvature. Do not stop at these equations: prove an original-scale payoff on complete words, or show the relevant scope where this mechanism loses it.

Other initial points are allowed if their actual interior/exterior transitions and original closest distance are proved. In particular, do not use v0^2 as the radius when x0 has positive first coordinate; there the closest point on the ray is0. Scaling the disk/coordinates does not authorize changing the physical steps during a run.

## Decisive original-scale test and then universal closure

For each enclosing m>=1536 retain eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt2)(1+eps), a=beta/c, H0 empty, H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), same parameters in every child. Let S_j=sum H_j, D_m=1+2S_m, n_m=2^m-1. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 gives B_n/D_m<47/50. R6_QUADRATIC_BASELINE_BOUND_20261009.md is an UNVERIFIED manual proof that the full quadratic pool is <=9/(16D_m); standard noncrossing Huber is1/D_m. That specified baseline pool is deficient, not necessarily every Huber crossing.

First establish an actual nonlinear terminal payoff at the needed 1/B_n scale for this ENTIRE inherited-m family, with explicit initial points or a rigorously optimized choice and a uniform estimate. A finite m example, favorable intermediate gradient, or the formula above alone is not enough. If this family is viable, continue to a proved selection rule for arbitrary full words and the exact uniform target. Passing H_m alone does not solve the original goal.

For at most ONE genuinely different second line, a possible repair is K=conv(B(e1,1),w) with an exposed vertex w outside the disk and positive first component. This may provide a constant-gradient prefix without forcing a common normal on the later curved gradients. Prove the actual global projection geometry, all old trajectory constraints and complete minimum set; do not assume a free activation or omit the prefix radius. The family is only a lead, not an assigned valid gluing theorem. An alternative genuine repair addressing a proved loss is also permissible within the two-line cap.

## Prior scope to respect

R6 common-normal replenishment is dominated by a real tangent history plus standardHuber in its proved geometry; R6 rightmost-spine path selections are below4/(5D_m); R7 f(x)=x^2/2 on the positive halfline and mu*x^2/2 on the negative is dominated by quadratics; R7 one-sided Huber affine-terminal returns cannot beat1/D_m. None excludes this curved body automatically. Main bounded shared lookup did not locate a tangent-disk-family ceiling; it did find old triangle/radial-envelope failures and ball-projection repair lemmas for representation. # Frozen scope of the single independent readback

One child process, five wall seconds including import, construction, all checks,
and result writing. Fully charge the public ledger before starting the child.
Any error or timeout consumes the slot. No smoke test, retry, solver, search,
optimizer, rank computation, benchmark DP, installation, or subsequent mathematical
program is authorized. Administrative source/data hashing is not a proof.

Use only Python integer/Fraction pairs representing a+b sqrt(2). Independently
type the seven physical steps and twenty base multipliers from the frozen text.
Reconstruct L=diag(row sums)-Lambda, the original T, the exact base flow and full
8-by-8 remainder. Rebuild each of the seven p and fifty-six Y coefficient maps
from D1+sym(Lbar Tp+L(Y)T0), not the author's checker or saved residual flags.

Decode the 28 upper-triangular column-major svec prices: divide off-diagonals by
sqrt(2). Compare all supplied matrix entries. Test that exactly the 36 original
zero entries have strictly positive sign prices and all 14 box prices are zero.
Check every entry of the supplied unit-lower LDL product and the exact positivity
of its seven pivots. Do not trust supplied PSD/passed flags.

For every original variable retain full W, f, We and B. Check the original identity
-S=trace(Z B)+sum sigma Y-a^T f-b^T We. Compare this trace both to H:W with
H=VZV^T and to the sqrt(2)-weighted svec inner product. Check e^T W e=f^T T0 e,
which becomes the necessary common-null scalar equality when f=0. The lossless
congruence and the PSD consequence of e^T W e=0 are proved analytically.

Persist the complete result and full stdout/stderr. The executable handles exact
failures by saving the first failed check; the external guard records timeouts.
The readback can validate this finite algebraic object but is no formal gate.
Part B equality-kernel and nonlinear-neighborhood work is textual reasoning only.

# Full seven-step tangent discovery: exact dual candidate

**Status: complete joint UNVERIFIED research report.** All five authorized
jobs are consumed and the ledger is closed. There was no fact submission,
formal verification, installation, benchmark replay or subagent. The original
unrestricted gradient-descent exact-profile problem remains OPEN.

The useful output is an explicit dual over Q(sqrt(2)) for the COMPLETE
first-order cone at the frozen balanced seven-step word. The recorded exact
reconstruction satisfies all63 stationarity equations, all36 sign-price
inequalities, and an exact positive-definite LDL decomposition. It gives
the following candidate conclusion:

> Every full linearized certificate (p,Y,W) satisfies sum_i p_i <= 0.
> This conclusion covers all seven physical derivatives, all56 original
> coefficient derivatives, all36 original active signs and full R8 PSD.

This is a proof candidate with explicit algebraic evidence, not an accepted
fact. No full-neighborhood, global seven-step or original-target theorem is
claimed. In particular the discovery supplies no improving finite seed.

## 1. Inputs, exact model and scope

The active assignment is TASK.md, ONE round after completed47, completed
ceiling48/attempted49, under guidance06cd713a43322da8. The shared preceding
draft b937bb05ba1645b1 has SHA256
76a363742a60cdab32d14b828dcf7ecd58d784dfcededfd1b9510b131d67565b.
It identifies this exact cone but does not solve it. Its base uniqueness,
contact and integration claims are not imported as accepted facts here.

Accepted04219554e41d9750, Sections10--11, supplies the original full
primitive identity and composition. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 fixes the
interpolation orientation. Accepted5e1848ee0b153f5f supplies conditional
amplification of an actual above-threshold seed only. None asserts such
a seed exists. All additional identities required below are derived jointly.

Let q=sqrt(2), l=1+q, u=q-1, and

    hbar=(q,2,q,1+l,q,2,q), Abar=l^3.

Index the eight independent formal gradient coordinates by0,...,7. Let
e be their all-ones column and let e_i be the standard coordinate columns.
For any zero-diagonal8-by8 array X, put L(X)=diag(Xe)-X. Define T0 to have
unit diagonal, entry hbar_j at i,j when j<i, and zero entries above its
diagonal. The base array has these twenty positive entries, with every
unlisted entry zero:

    01:2                  10:2l                 13:2l
    21:2lq                23:2l^2+2lq           31:2l
    32:2l^3               37:2l^2               43:2lq
    45:2l^2               47:2lq                53:4l
    54:2l^3               57:2l^3+4l            63:2lq
    65:2l^3q              67:2l^4+2l^3q+2lq
    73:2l                 75:2l^3               76:2l^5.

Call this array Lambdabar and write Lbar=L(Lambdabar). Let Z0 be the
set of the other36 ordered distinct pairs. The complete variable array
Y has Y_ii=0 and all56 other entries independently real before constraints.
For p in R7 set S=sum p_i and define

    Tp_ij=p_j for j<i, zero otherwise;
    op=(-2p_0,...,-2p_6,2S);
    D1=diag(2(1-hbar_i)p_i for i<7,-2Abar S);
    W=D1+sym(Lbar Tp+L(Y)T0), sym(K)=(K+K^T)/2;
    f=L(Y)^T e-op, n=We.

The full cone consists of f=0, Y_ij>=0 for ij in Z0 and W>=0 on R8.
The other twenty Y entries have no imposed sign. No support, rank,
reflection, equality of formal gradients or fixed shape of W is assumed.

## 2. Exact common null and lossless PSD representation

For an arbitrary word h, s=sum h_i, and full flow L^Te=(-2h,2s), put
M=D+sym(LT), D=diag(h_i(2-h_i),-s(s+2)), with T the original prefix
matrix. Since the i-th entry of Te is1+sum_(j<i)h_j,

    omega^T Te=s^2+sum h_i^2,
    e^T D e=-s^2-sum h_i^2,

so e^TMe=0. Along hbar+t p and Lambdabar+tY, the flow equation is
exact whenever f=0, and differentiation gives e^TWe=0. A symmetric PSD
matrix with zero quadratic form at e annihilates e: its quadratic form
at e+t z cannot be nonnegative for all real t unless z^TWe=0 for each z.
Thus n=0 is a necessary full-cone equation.

Let V=[I7;-1...-1], G=V^TV=I7+11^T and B=V^TWV. Its columns span
e-perp. When n=0, every vector is a sum of a multiple of e and a vector
Vz, so W>=0 on R8 if and only if B>=0 on R7. Moreover

    W>=m(I8-ee^T/8)  iff  B>=mG.

Consequently adding all8 n=0 equations and imposing this congruent PSD
condition loses no original solution. The common-null direction never
receives an epsilon perturbation. Raw readbacks contain the original
full W, every original flow equation, and the full null residual.

## 3. Both calibrations established before job1

For the singleton physical step x, choose multipliers2 and2(1+x) on the
two directed pairs. Substitution into the original two-gradient identity
gives the full remainder

    (2-x^2)(e_0-e_1)(e_0-e_1)^T.

It is nonnegative when0<=x<=q. Set x=q-t,0<=t<q. Join identical
singleton children using bridge1+u(1+x), then identical three-step
children using bridge2+x. Their respective capacities are1+x,l(1+x),
and l^2(1+x). Each balanced join has right multiplier factor l^2 and
additional interpolation factor l^2-1=2l. The accepted full join identity
therefore proves feasibility without a computation. The complete array
and its derivative are specified in frozen_model.md and in the exact
calibration artifact.

The derivative is p=(-1,-u,-1,-1,-1,-u,-1), with S=-l^2. If
d_ij=e_i-e_j, its exact remainder derivative is

    W=2q(d01 d01^T+l^2 d23 d23^T+l^2 d45 d45^T+l^4 d67 d67^T).

Every active zero-entry derivative is zero. This is a NONZERO positive
calibration from an actual feasible path. Job1 recorded exact base flow,
base M=0, derivative flow, null, all36 signs and the displayed rank-one
sum identity, then numerically solved the full fixed calibration.

For the excluded direction, v(h)=T(h)^(-1)e obeys v_0=1 and
v_(i+1)=(1-h_i)v_i. Since L e=0,

    v^T Mv=v^TDv=1-A(h)^2 v_7^2.

At the base M=0 and Abar^2 vbar_7^2=1. Differentiating yields

    vbar^T W vbar=-2[S/l^3+l(p0+p2+p4+p6)+p1+p5+up3].

The direction p=(1,...,1) has strictly negative right side
-2(7/l^3+4l+2+u), so it cannot satisfy full PSD for any Y. This analytic
contradiction was frozen before the negative numerical calibration.

## 4. Frozen jobs and observations

Before any mathematical import or evaluation, frozen_model.md,
tangent_jobs.py and run_job.py were hashed in freeze.json. The numerical
implementation uses the already published shared Python runtime and direct
Clarabel cone data. The [official Clarabel SDP example](https://clarabel.org/stable/examples/py/example_sdp/)
specifies the upper-triangular, column-major svec convention, with q on
off-diagonal entries. The [Python interface documentation](https://clarabel.org/stable/python/getting_started_py/)
specifies the complete primal/dual return arrays. These are implementation
sources; no external mathematical theorem is used as a proof premise.

All p coordinates are bounded by |p_i|<=1 during the two discovery SDPs.
No other variable is bounded. The strict problem maximizes m subject to
S>=m, all36 Y_zero>=m, f=n=0 and B>=mG. If the frozen credibility test
fails, the nonstrict problem maximizes S subject to the complete cone.
All options, thresholds and reconstruction choices are in the freeze.

|Job|Action|Recorded status/result|External process seconds|
|---|---|---|---|
|1|Exact nonzero calibration and feasibility SDP|Exact identities passed; `AlmostSolved`; original equality residual3.893774191965349e-12|2.8775796109985095|
|2|Excluded p=(1,...,1) calibration SDP|`PrimalInfeasible`; complete dual ray passed frozen readback|1.7735932099894853|
|3|Free-p strict-margin SDP|`AlmostSolved`; m=-1.7373600391814525e-9|1.7222137329954421|
|4|Free-p nonstrict capacity SDP|`AlmostSolved`; S=-5.056399669088169e-8|1.722976656004903|
|5|One exact homogeneous dual reconstruction|All63 exact stationarity residuals zero;36 sign prices nonnegative; exact dual LDL positive definite|0.36915727599989623|

The job3 numerical gap was2.547742288815272e-9; its smallest recorded
complement eigenvalue was-1.420580825145002e-9. It does not supply a
strict tangent. Job4's numerical gap was7.87119183312904e-8; its original
equality residual was8.644757023430468e-13. Neither of these numerical
outcomes by itself proves a bound. Their raw dual object was only a lead
for the exact reconstruction in Section5.

All five jobs returned process code0; no timeout or repair occurred.
`AlmostSolved` is a reduced-accuracy status, preserved without relabeling.
The positive calibration's acceptance follows the frozen tolerance rule
and independent exact calibration formulas, not a claim of exactness for
the floating solver point. The negative calibration also had its exact
moment contradiction before solving.

Each job was charged the FULL8 seconds before launch and externally
limited to8 seconds, covering imports, model construction, solve,
readback and writes. Total accounted usage is40 seconds. Total measured
external process time is8.465520485988236 seconds. Internal solver and
kernel timers and administrative times are recorded separately in raw
files and the ledger. No unmeasured transport time is presented as kernel
time. No credit is refunded or transferred. All remaining jobs are zero.

All original primal and dual arrays, dense conic data, index maps,
settings, stdout/stderr, statuses, gaps, eigendiagnostics and return codes
are public. Symmetric matrices are stored literally symmetric. Numerical
eigenvalues came only from real symmetric eigensolvers, with no discarded
imaginary part and no numerical Cholesky. The reconstruction uses exact
LDL with explicit nonpositive/zero-pivot checks.

## 5. The exact dual and the candidate proof

Job5 rounded free coordinates once to multiples of2^-20 and solved exact
affine equations in the field Q(q), q^2=2. All p-box prices were set to
zero as a reconstruction ansatz. This was not a restriction of either SDP.
No optimization or parameter grid was used in reconstruction.

The appendix specifies two eight-coordinate vectors a,b (the first and
last halves of `equality_prices`), a symmetric7-by7 matrix Z through its
28 svec entries, and36 coefficients sigma_ij. All their displayed numbers
are exact rational pairs (r,s), meaning r+s q. Off-diagonal svec entries
are q Z_ij, and diagonal entries are Z_ii.

The algebraic identity certified by the63 zero stationarity residuals is

    -S = trace(Z B)+sum_(ij in Z0) sigma_ij Y_ij
         -a^T f-b^T n.                                      (D)

This is an identity for EVERY p and EVERY zero-diagonal real Y, before
any cone constraint. There is no p-normalization term because all14 box
prices are exactly zero.

Here is an explicit way to check every coefficient of(D) without a
solver. Set H=VZV^T and K=H-sym(b e^T). Write t_i for the transpose of
the i-th row of T0 and r_ij=t_i-t_j. A single unit Y_ij contributes
e_i-e_j to f and sym(e_i r_ij^T) to W. Thus the56 required identities are

    a_i-a_j-(K r_ij)_i = 0              on the20 base-positive pairs,
    a_i-a_j-(K r_ij)_i = sigma_ij       on the36 other pairs. (Y)

For each i<7 let C_i have entry1 in column i strictly below its diagonal,
and zero elsewhere. The unit p_i contribution to W is

    N_i=2(1-hbar_i)e_i e_i^T-2Abar e_7e_7^T+sym(Lbar C_i).

Its contribution to f is2e_i-2e_7. The seven remaining identities are

    2(a_i-a_7)-trace(K N_i)=1, i=0,...,6.                  (P)

The appendixed numbers and q^2=2 specify all entries in(Y) and(P).
The reconstruction artifact records each of their63 residuals as the
exact pair(0,0), using the equivalent unsimplified original coefficient
map. It retains the exact source and deterministic arithmetic procedure.

All36 sigma values are nonnegative in the real embedding q>0, and none
is zero. The following elementary rule makes their comparisons exact:
if r,s have the same sign, use that sign; if they have opposite signs,
the sign of r+s q is sign(r) times sign(r^2-2s^2). The cases r=0 or
s=0 are immediate. The nonzero opposite-sign case cannot yield zero,
because q is irrational. This is the precise rule used by the frozen
exact arithmetic, with rational operations only.

For Z, the artifact supplies Z=L diag(d_0,...,d_6)L^T with unit lower
triangular L and all seven pivots d_i strictly positive under that same
sign rule. The appendix includes L and all seven exact pairs. The LDL
recurrence is

    d_k=Z_kk-sum_(j<k)L_kj^2 d_j,
    L_ik=(Z_ik-sum_(j<k)L_ij L_kj d_j)/d_k, i>k.

These formulas prove that the supplied decomposition entails Z>0, with
no floating eigenvalue inference. All pivots were tested exactly before
division, and a zero pivot with nonzero remaining column would have been
rejected. The recorded result has no zero pivot.

For a full feasible tangent, f=n=0, every Y_ij in Z0 is nonnegative and
B>=0. If Z=L diag(d)L^T, then
trace(ZB)=sum_k d_k (L e_k)^T B(L e_k)>=0. Applying(D) gives -S>=0.
This proves the candidate first-order capacity bound from the explicit
algebraic checks. A zero tangent attains S=0, so the normalized nonstrict
problem has exact optimum0 at draft level. The strict model likewise has
optimum0: S>=m and S<=0 imply m<=0, and the zero point with m=0 is feasible.

The same identity also gives a useful equality condition: if S=0, each
active Y entry is zero, and B=0 because Z is positive definite. To see
the latter without a spectral theorem, each nonnegative summand
(L e_k)^T B(L e_k) must vanish; PSD then makes B annihilate the basis
formed by the columns of L. Consequently W=0 as well. This does not
by itself settle how a finite higher-order perturbation can behave.

## 6. What this closes and what remains

At candidate level this resolves the specific missing lifted tangent
interface in b937bb05ba1645b1: its contact-surviving vector
p_test=(0,0,0,0,0,2,-1), with S=1, admits NO full first-order completion
Y. The obstruction is independent of the attempted W_test, any skew
parameterization or any support choice. It applies to all W and Y in
the original linearized cone.

The proof does not establish a neighborhood capacity bound. The exact
finite remainder still contains

    D2(p)+sym(L(Y)Tp),
    D2(p)=diag(-p_0^2,...,-p_6^2,-S^2).

A finite feasible perturbation may have zero first-order capacity change,
singular leading terms, or coefficient difference quotients not known
to be bounded. A separate argument must control these terms and all
nearby full certificates. The reconstructed equality condition above
is a concrete input for such a future argument, after verification.
The reconstruction also records affine elimination rank27; this is
preserved as finite algebraic telemetry, not promoted here to an
additional coercivity or neighborhood theorem.

No full strict positive-capacity tangent was found, so the sufficient
integration construction was not invoked and no finite seed parameter
was selected. A small numerical residual or an infeasibility label is
never used as a mathematical conclusion. The original target is still
OPEN, and the only deliverable eligible for a future formal gate is the
joint algebraic tangent theorem with all its explicit checks.

returned copositive-tensor and distributed-system statements with different

claim. No new evaluation is permitted after the closed ledger.

# Independent exact tangent audit and a full local capacity theorem

Status: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No fact submission or formal
gate was allocated or used. The exact arithmetic readback is an independently
implemented finite check, not verifier acceptance. Every new lemma and the local
theorem below belong to this one joint draft; no earlier unverified finding is
used as an accepted premise. The original all-word exact-profile goal is OPEN.
